# IBRiS/Rzeczpospolita Parliamentary Voting Intention — Latest Data (as of 27 July 2026)

## Most Recent Poll: Fieldwork 24–25 July 2026 (published ~27 July 2026)

The newest IBRiS poll for *Rzeczpospolita* (rp.pl) was conducted **24–25 July 2026**, method **CATI**, sample **n=1,000**. Crucially, this poll was fielded **during the PiS split** — the days when Mateusz Morawiecki and his "Rozwój Plus" association were leaving/being expelled from PiS. The published "gdyby wybory do Sejmu" figures **include Rozwój Plus as a separate party**, which pulled roughly 6 pp away from PiS:

| Party | Support | Seats (of 460) | Change vs early July |
|---|---|---|---|
| **KO** | **30.1%** | 186 | +1.0 pp |
| **PiS** | **17.3%** | 98 | −6.0 pp (Morawiecki split) |
| Konfederacja | 12.3% | 64 | −1.0 pp |
| Konfederacja Korony Polskiej (Braun) | 10.9% | 54 | −0.1 pp |
| Rozwój Plus (Morawiecki) | 7.4% | 30 | new |
| Nowa Lewica | 7.0% | 28 | −1.4 pp |
| PSL | 2.8% | — | below threshold |
| Razem | 1.6% | — | — |
| Polska 2050 | 0.5% | — | — |

Declared turnout dropped to ~51.4% (from ~60.4% in early July).

## Important clarification on the "PiS 28.7%" figure in your context

**The "late-July KO 30.1 vs PiS 28.7" reading in your brief appears to be a conflation.** The verified IBRiS/*Rzeczpospolita* poll of 24–25 July shows **KO 30.1% and PiS 17.3%** — because it was taken during the Morawiecki split and reported PiS *without* the ~7.4% now attributed to Rozwój Plus. Adding Rozwój Plus back (17.3 + 7.4 ≈ 24.7) still does not reach 28.7%. I could not confirm any IBRiS/Rzeczpospolita variant reporting PiS at exactly 28.7% in late July. The "28.7" may originate from a different pollster or a mis-transcription. **The confirmed KO figure (30.1%) matches; the PiS figure should be read as 17.3% in the published July 24–25 poll.**

## Recent IBRiS/Rzeczpospolita trajectory (KO vs PiS)

- **Late May 2026:** KO 30.9% vs PiS 25.4% (your context; consistent with sources)
- **Early July (2–3 July 2026):** KO 29.1% (168 seats) vs PiS ~23.2–23.3% — *"KO problems, only the far right gains, PiS has a growing problem"*
- **Late July (24–25 July 2026):** KO 30.1% (186 seats) vs PiS 17.3% (98 seats), with Rozwój Plus 7.4% new

## Publication cadence
IBRiS polls for *Rzeczpospolita* run on a roughly **monthly cadence**, typically published on a **Wednesday**, with fieldwork over two consecutive days (e.g., 27–28 Feb, 27–28 Mar, late May, 2–3 July, 24–25 July). Note the late-July poll was an off-cycle addition prompted by the PiS split. As of 27 July 2026, **no August 2026 IBRiS/Rzeczpospolita poll has been published** — the 24–25 July poll is the most recent.

*(Note: separate United Surveys by IBRiS polls for Wirtualna Polska, also fielded 24–25 July, showed KO 29.4%/PiS 21.5% without Morawiecki and KO 28.5%/PiS 15.9% with Rozwój Plus at 7.5% — these are a different commissioner and should not be confused with the rp.pl series.)*

I have gathered strong, consistent evidence on the IBRiS/Rzeczpospolita polling cadence for 2026.

### Key finding: IBRiS produces a monthly "partyjny" (party support) poll for *Rzeczpospolita*, published mid-week (Wednesday). Critically, a NEW IBRiS/Rzeczpospolita poll was ALREADY conducted July 24–25, 2026 (published ~July 27), falling squarely inside the July 27–August 7 window's opening.

Let me note the important nuance for the forecast window: the July 24–25 poll was published right around July 27. The question is whether *another* IBRiS/Rzeczpospolita poll appears Jul 27–Aug 7.

### Established pattern (IBRiS for Rzeczpospolita, "partyjny" monthly series):
- **Late May 2026** – IBRiS/Rz poll (referenced as "koniec maja")
- **June 16–17, 2026** – IBRiS/Rz poll
- **July 2–3, 2026** – IBRiS/Rz poll (published Wed July 8), KO 29.1%, PiS 23.2%
- **July 24–25, 2026** – IBRiS/Rz poll (published ~July 27), testing Morawiecki's "Rozwój Plus" — KO 30.1%, PiS 17.3%, Rozwój Plus 7.4%

This shows the standard monthly "partyjny" cadence (late May → mid-June → early July), plus an *extra* special July 24–25 poll triggered by the PiS split / Morawiecki news event.

### Implication for July 27 – August 7, 2026 window:
- The regular monthly poll for *this cycle* was just published (July 24–25 fieldwork, ~July 27 publication) — this is the most recent IBRiS/Rz release.
- Following the monthly cadence, the *next regularly scheduled* IBRiS/Rz "partyjny" poll would normally fall in **late August 2026** (fieldwork ~mid-to-late August), i.e., OUTSIDE the July 27–Aug 7 window.
- However, IBRiS is highly responsive to news events (the July 24–25 poll itself was event-driven by the PiS/Morawiecki split, which is ongoing per July 27 reporting). Additional event-driven polls during active political turmoil cannot be ruled out.

Note: IBRiS also runs a *separate* United Surveys by IBRiS series for Wirtualna Polska (WP) and a series for Polsat News — these are distinct clients and should not be confused with the Rzeczpospolita series.

I now have a clear and critical picture. The GazetaPrawna source confirms the 24-25 July IBRiS/Rzeczpospolita poll headline is **KO 30.1% vs PiS 17.3%** (a +12.8pp lead) — because it "uwzględnił hipotetyczne ugrupowanie Morawieckiego" (included Morawiecki's hypothetical Rozwój Plus party, 7.4%, split out from PiS). The question author's "PiS 28.7%" appears to be an error/conflation with other pollsters. Let me record findings and check whether there's a baseline scenario and the timing question.

```json
}
```

```
REVIEWER FAILED: **Verdict rationale: the tentative center (10.5) is meaningfully too low; it should be ~12.5.** The downside fork that drags the median down rests on a resolver interpretation the
resolution criteria explicitly exclude.

**What I verified in the trace:**
- The core headline claim (Factor 1) is well-grounded. The rp.pl article (the primary/authoritative resolution source) reports the standard "gdyby wybory do Sejmu" Voting Intention table as **KO 30.1%
/ PiS 17.3% → +12.8pp**, corroborated by GazetaPrawna, dorzeczy.pl, and wp.pl. No hallucination; Factor 1's +2.0 logit is correct in sign.
- Factor 4 (author's +1.4pp premise is a factual error / conflation with United Surveys ~28) is also supported by the trace.
- Resolution criteria are PROVIDED and detailed; no recovery failure. The counterargument is a genuine alternative story (not a token gesture). Tool_audit narrative is broadly consistent with the
metrics.

**The core error — Factor 2 / Monte Carlo overweight a resolver-invalid "baseline" fork:**
Factor 2 claims 17.3 is a "HYPOTHETICAL scenario" and that a resolver "treating the standard/baseline (Rozwój Plus reabsorbed) would record PiS ~23-25 → lead ~5-7," assigning it ~38% weight inside the
no-new-poll branch. The trace directly contradicts the premise:
- The agent's own fetch_url result states: **"Only ONE variant is reported (WITH Rozwój Plus)... The content does not show PiS at 28.7% in any variant."** There is NO published baseline PiS figure in
the rp.pl/Rzeczpospolita poll.
- The baseline variants (PiS ~21.5 without Morawiecki) exist ONLY in the WP/United Surveys-by-IBRiS series — a **different commissioner that the resolution fine print explicitly disregards.**
- The resolution criteria say to use "the headline party voting-intention percentages **as reported**" on rp.pl. As reported = 17.3. Rozwój Plus being listed as a separate party in the standard "gdyby
wybory" poll does not make PiS's reported figure hypothetical; 17.3 is the headline. The Wikipedia backup would likewise log the poll's reported PiS figure (17.3), not an invented reabsorption.

Thus a resolver mechanically computes 30.1 − 17.3 = **+12.8**. The agent's own findings file even concedes: "rp.pl article ONLY presented the 17.3 scenario → strongly favors +12.8." Yet the Monte
Carlo still puts ~38% of the no-new-poll mass on a ~6.3 baseline cluster, pulling the median from 12.8 down to 10.5 and setting P10 at 4.6.

**My independent estimate:** The only genuine uncertainty is whether a NEW IBRiS/Rz poll publishes before Aug 7 (~28% is reasonable — the 24-25 Jul poll was off-cycle/event-driven; next scheduled
~late Aug). Conditional on no new poll (~72%), the answer is essentially exactly 12.8. In the new-poll branch, the fractured right persists near-term, so a new poll most likely still shows an elevated
lead (~9-11) with real spread. This yields a distribution heavily concentrated at 12.8 with a modest lower tail — roughly P10 ~7, P50 ~12.5, P90 ~13.2 — NOT a symmetric bimodal split with a cluster at
6. The agent is over-weighting a resolver-interpretation fork the criteria exclude and double-counting downside uncertainty already present in the new-poll branch.

**Recommendation:** Remove (or drastically down-weight to <10%) the "baseline reabsorption" fork, since no such figure is published in the qualifying series and the criteria disregard the commissioner
that does publish it. Recenter the distribution to median ~12.5 with the bulk of mass at 12.8 and the lower tail driven almost entirely by the ~28% new-poll probability.

Fix the issues above, then call premortem() again.
```

## Summary of checks

**Factor 1 (+12.8pp, the primary resolver) — fully grounded.** The direct rp.pl fetch (art44892171) confirms KO 30.1% / PiS 17.3% as the sole reported voting-intention table, corroborated by dorzeczy, GazetaPrawna, TVN24, and polsatnews. 30.1 − 17.3 = 12.8. This is the "headline as reported" figure on the authoritative resolution source. No hallucination.

**Factor 2 (baseline disregarded) — correct.** The ~21.5 PiS baseline appears only in the United Surveys-by-IBRiS/WP series, which the fine print explicitly excludes (different commissioner). The trace and worldview research cleanly separate the two series. The negative logit correctly down-weights this resolver-invalid fork after the premortem.

**Factor 3 (~28% new-poll branch) — reasonable.** Cadence research shows monthly cadence, next regular poll ~late August (outside window), 24-25 July was off-cycle/event-driven, no August poll as of 27 July. The 28% is a defensible blend of "not scheduled" and "IBRiS runs event-driven polls during an active split."

**Factor 4 (author premise error) — correct.** The question's PiS 28.7/+1.4pp premise is indeed unverifiable; the agent correctly identified it as a conflation.

**No double-counting, no wrong-direction signs, no contradictory assessment.** The premortem correction (down-weighting the baseline fork, recentering to 12.6) is documented and improved calibration.

**Resolution alignment — strong.** Factors engage the specific criteria terms (rp.pl authoritative source, "as reported" headline, commissioner exclusion, fieldwork-date recency, June 1–Aug 7 annulment window).

**Tool audit — no false claims.** The narrative doesn't claim "no failures"; ground-truth errors (research 33%, premortem 25%, polymarket 100%) aren't contradicted.

**Independent probability estimate — I concur.** Conditional on no new poll (~72%), the value is essentially exactly 12.8; the ~28% branch adds a legitimate lower tail. Median ~12.6, P10 ~6.0, P90 ~13.2 is well-calibrated. The confidence of 0.55 is appropriately humble given the genuine new-poll uncertainty, and the counterargument constructs a real alternative story (new poll + resolver reinterpretation). No meaningful divergence from my own estimate.

Minor note: the ~15% residual resolver-judgment fork is arguably slightly generous given how literal the "as reported" criterion is, but this is a magnitude quibble that doesn't affect direction or verdict.

## Summary

**Question:** KO's lead over PiS in the last IBRiS/Rzeczpospolita poll published before Aug 8, 2026.

**Key finding:** The question author's premise was factually wrong. The author stated the late-July reading was KO 30.1% vs PiS 28.7% (+1.4pp), but the actual latest IBRiS/Rzeczpospolita poll (fieldwork 24-25 July 2026) reports **KO 30.1% vs PiS 17.3% = +12.8pp**. PiS collapsed because Morawiecki's new "Rozwój Plus" party (7.4%) was split out following the PiS split. The 28.7% figure was a conflation with other pollsters (United Surveys ~28%, IPSOS ~28%).

**Critical resolution insight:** The ~21.5% "baseline" PiS figure exists only in the WP/United Surveys-by-IBRiS series, which the fine print **explicitly disregards**. The qualifying rp.pl series publishes only the 17.3% variant, so "headline as reported" = 17.3 → +12.8pp.

**Distribution:** Heavily concentrated at +12.8 (median 12.6), with a lower tail (P10 ~6.0) driven mainly by the ~28% chance a new IBRiS/Rz poll publishes before Aug 7. The open upper bound was essential — most probability mass sits at or above the author's stated +10 ceiling.

- **Median:** +12.6 pp
- **P10–P90:** +6.0 to +13.2 pp

The premortem gate caught and corrected an initial over-weighting of a resolver-invalid "baseline" fork, recentering the distribution appropriately before final submission.
